# CCEA GCE Further Mathematics Specification Map
**Source 1:** GCE Further Mathematics (2018)-specification-Standard_2.pdf  
**Source 2:** GCE Further Mathematics Elaboration Document.pdf  
**Date generated:** 2026-06-02  
**Purpose:** Master curriculum map for CCEA AS/A Level Further Mathematics revision project.

---
## 1. How to Use This Document
This document is organised as a specification-first curriculum map. The official CCEA Further Mathematics Specification supplies the unit structure, assessment structure and learning outcomes. The Further Mathematics Elaboration Document supplies examples, limits, interpretation notes and explicit inclusions or exclusions where they are available.

For lesson planning, start with the unit overview and then use each topic summary and expanded learning outcome note to decide what a lesson must cover. For question-bank tagging, use the stable LO IDs. For revision tracking, copy the tracker in Section 10 and mark each line as Not started, In progress, Complete or Needs review. For visual planning, use the visual/widget column as a prompt only; it is not extra syllabus content.

Project unit codes in this document use an `F` prefix to avoid collisions with the separate CCEA GCE Mathematics map: `FAS1`, `FAS2`, `FA21` and `FA22`. The official unit names remain AS 1, AS 2, A2 1 and A2 2.

---
## 4. Overarching Themes
### Mathematical language, notation and set language
**Official specification idea:** Use precise mathematical language and syntax, including equality, identity, implication, equivalence, necessary and sufficient conditions, and set notation such as complement, empty set, intersection, union and membership.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Further Mathematics students must be able to write technical mathematics without ambiguity. This matters especially in proof, group theory, graph theory, matrices, statistics and differential equations.

**Where it commonly appears:** Across all four Further Mathematics units, with topic-specific emphasis.

**Project tags:** `#MathematicalLanguage`, `#Notation`, `#SetTheory`, `#Communication`

### Proof, critique and reasoning
**Official specification idea:** Construct, present, comprehend and critique mathematical arguments, proofs and justifications of methods and formulae.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Students need to explain why a method works, not only carry it out. This is especially explicit in induction, De Moivre, vertical circular motion proofs and theorem-based Decision Mathematics.

**Where it commonly appears:** Across all four Further Mathematics units, with topic-specific emphasis.

**Project tags:** `#Proof`, `#Reasoning`, `#Justification`, `#Induction`

### Problem solving and structure
**Official specification idea:** Recognise underlying mathematical structure, simplify and abstract appropriately, and construct extended arguments for unstructured problems.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Further Maths questions often hide the route. Students should learn to classify the structure first: matrix system, vector geometry, recurrence relation, distribution model, differential equation or mechanics model.

**Where it commonly appears:** Across all four Further Mathematics units, with topic-specific emphasis.

**Project tags:** `#ProblemSolving`, `#ClassifyStructure`, `#ExtendedArgument`

### Modelling, assumptions and interpretation
**Official specification idea:** Translate contexts into mathematical models, make simplifying assumptions, use the model, interpret outputs and evaluate whether the model is appropriate.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Mechanics and statistics depend on assumptions. Students should state what is being modelled and check whether the answer makes sense in context.

**Where it commonly appears:** Across all four Further Mathematics units, with topic-specific emphasis.

**Project tags:** `#Modelling`, `#Assumptions`, `#InterpretContext`, `#EvaluateModel`

### Diagrams, graphs and visual reasoning
**Official specification idea:** Extract information from diagrams, construct mathematical diagrams, sketch graphs and use diagrams to solve problems.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Argand diagrams, 3D vector diagrams, force diagrams, polar graphs, graph-theory diagrams and statistical plots are core working tools.

**Where it commonly appears:** Across all four Further Mathematics units, with topic-specific emphasis.

**Project tags:** `#Diagram`, `#SketchGraph`, `#Argand`, `#ForceDiagram`, `#GraphTheory`

### Technology and calculators
**Official specification idea:** Technology, especially mathematical/statistical graphing tools and spreadsheets, should permeate teaching. Calculators must include an iterative function, matrix calculations up to at least order $3\times3$, summary statistics and standard distribution probabilities. Computer algebra systems are not allowed in examinations.

**Student-friendly meaning:** Use technology to explore, check and visualise, while still learning manual methods and exam-acceptable reasoning.

**Where it commonly appears:** Across all four Further Mathematics units, with topic-specific emphasis.

**Project tags:** `#UseCalculator`, `#UseSpreadsheet`, `#GraphingTool`, `#NoCAS`

### Synoptic A2 thinking
**Official specification idea:** A2 units include synoptic assessment: building on AS material and connecting areas of knowledge, understanding and skills across the course.

**Student-friendly meaning:** A2 Further Mathematics is not a set of isolated techniques. Differential equations may rely on algebra and complex roots; mechanics may require calculus, vectors and modelling; statistics may require distribution knowledge and careful interpretation.

**Where it commonly appears:** Across all four Further Mathematics units, with topic-specific emphasis.

**Project tags:** `#Synoptic`, `#Connections`, `#A2`

## 6. Unit-by-Unit Specification Map
Each unit below contains a unit overview, topic summaries, learning outcome tables and expanded notes. LO IDs are stable project identifiers for lesson notes, question banks, revision trackers and evidence maps.

---
# FAS1: Pure Mathematics (AS 1)
## Unit Overview
**Role in course:** Compulsory pure unit for both AS and A level Further Mathematics.

**Assessment details:** External written examination; students answer all questions; 6-10 questions worth 100 raw marks.

**Prerequisite / assumption note:** Assumes Unit AS 1 of CCEA GCE Mathematics.

**Broad content areas:** Further algebra and functions, complex numbers, matrices and vectors.

**Important student-facing comments:** This unit is the bridge from single-subject Mathematics into Further Mathematics: it introduces complex arithmetic, matrix methods and three-dimensional vector geometry.

**Links to overarching themes:** mathematical language, proof/reasoning, modelling, diagrams, technology, communication and synoptic connections.

## Topic: Further algebra and functions

### Topic Summary

Roots and coefficients of quadratic equations, including forming new quadratics from related roots.

### Learning Outcomes Table

### Expanded Learning Outcome Notes

#### FAS1-FAF-LO001: Use Vieta-style relationships between the roots and coefficients of a quadratic

**Official specification wording:**  
demonstrate understanding of and use the relationship between roots and coefficients of quadratic equations

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
The elaboration expects students to work with roots such as $\alpha$ and $\beta$ of a quadratic polynomial and evaluate expressions including $\alpha^2+\beta^2$, $\alpha^3+\beta^3$, $1/\alpha+1/\beta$ and $(2+\alpha)(2+\beta)$.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Use Vieta-style relationships between the roots and coefficients of a quadratic.

**What students should be able to do:**
- translate between a quadratic equation and the sum/product of its roots
- rewrite expressions in the roots using algebra before substituting values
- form a monic or non-monic quadratic from a known root sum and product

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary and apply the elaboration guidance exactly. Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
AS Mathematics quadratics, factorising, expanding brackets and algebraic fractions.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#FAS1`, `#FAF`, `#FurtherAlgebra`, `#RootsCoefficients`, `#WorkedSolution`, `#ExamTechnique`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
Root-coefficient triangle linking equation, root sum and root product.

**Links to other learning outcomes:**  
FAS1-FAF-LO002


**Source reference:**  
Specification: p. 9  
Elaboration Document: p. 1

#### FAS1-FAF-LO002: Build a new quadratic when its roots are described in terms of the original roots

**Official specification wording:**  
form a quadratic equation whose roots are related to the roots of a given quadratic equation

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
The elaboration expects students to work with roots such as $\alpha$ and $\beta$ of a quadratic polynomial and evaluate expressions including $\alpha^2+\beta^2$, $\alpha^3+\beta^3$, $1/\alpha+1/\beta$ and $(2+\alpha)(2+\beta)$.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Build a new quadratic when its roots are described in terms of the original roots.

**What students should be able to do:**
- translate between a quadratic equation and the sum/product of its roots
- rewrite expressions in the roots using algebra before substituting values
- form a monic or non-monic quadratic from a known root sum and product

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary and apply the elaboration guidance exactly. Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
AS Mathematics quadratics, factorising, expanding brackets and algebraic fractions.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#FAS1`, `#FAF`, `#FurtherAlgebra`, `#RootsCoefficients`, `#WorkedSolution`, `#ExamTechnique`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
Root-coefficient triangle linking equation, root sum and root product.

**Links to other learning outcomes:**  
FAS1-FAF-LO001


**Source reference:**  
Specification: p. 9  
Elaboration Document: p. 1

### Expanded Learning Outcome Notes

#### FAS1-CN-LO001: Solve any quadratic equation with real coefficients

**Official specification wording:**  
solve any quadratic equation with real coefficients

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
No additional elaboration guidance found in the supplied Elaboration Document for this individual learning outcome.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Solve any quadratic equation with real coefficients

**What students should be able to do:**
- move between algebraic and geometric interpretations of complex numbers
- use conjugate-pair reasoning to complete polynomial factors
- draw and interpret Argand diagrams with distances, angles and loci

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary. No extra elaboration guidance beyond the official outcome was identified for this individual line.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
AS Mathematics quadratics, polynomial factorisation, trigonometry, radians and coordinate geometry.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#FAS1`, `#CN`, `#ComplexNumbers`, `#Argand`, `#ModulusArgument`, `#WorkedSolution`, `#ExamTechnique`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
Argand diagram set showing addition, modulus-argument form and common loci.

FAS1-CN-LO002

#### FAS1-CN-LO002: Solve cubic or quartic equations with real coefficients, given sufficient information to deduce at least one root for cubics or at least one complex root or quadratic factor for quartics

**Official specification wording:**  
solve cubic or quartic equations with real coefficients, given sufficient information to deduce at least one root for cubics or at least one complex root or quadratic factor for quartics

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
The specification supplies the information threshold: students should not be expected to invent unsupported roots for cubics or quartics.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Solve cubic or quartic equations with real coefficients, given sufficient information to deduce at least one root for cubics or at least one complex root or quadratic factor for quartics

**What students should be able to do:**
- move between algebraic and geometric interpretations of complex numbers
- use conjugate-pair reasoning to complete polynomial factors
- draw and interpret Argand diagrams with distances, angles and loci

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary and apply the elaboration guidance exactly. Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
AS Mathematics quadratics, polynomial factorisation, trigonometry, radians and coordinate geometry.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#FAS1`, `#CN`, `#ComplexNumbers`, `#Argand`, `#ModulusArgument`, `#WorkedSolution`, `#ExamTechnique`

Matrix arithmetic, linear transformations, determinants, inverse matrices and matrix solution of simultaneous equations.

# FAS2: Applied Mathematics (AS 2)
## Unit Overview
**Role in course:** Compulsory applied unit for both AS and A level Further Mathematics, but students answer two specified sections.

**Assessment details:** External written examination; 6-10 questions worth 100 raw marks. Students answer two of four sections: A and B, A and C, A and D, or C and D. Each chosen section is worth 50% of this unit.

**Prerequisite / assumption note:** Assumes CCEA GCE Mathematics Units AS 1 and AS 2, plus Further Mathematics AS 1.

**Broad content areas:** Mechanics 1, Mechanics 2, Statistics, and Discrete and Decision Mathematics.

**Important student-facing comments:** Do not treat all four sections as simultaneously required for every learner. Mechanics 2 in this unit depends on Mechanics 1.

**Links to overarching themes:** mathematical language, proof/reasoning, modelling, diagrams, technology, communication and synoptic connections.

## Topic: Hooke's law


**Applied section:** Section A: Mechanics 1

### Topic Summary

Elastic strings and springs modelled through force, extension or compression and modulus of elasticity.

### Learning Outcomes Table

| LO ID | Specification learning outcome | Elaboration guidance | Student-friendly meaning | Required depth / boundaries | Suggested lesson tags | Suggested question-bank tags | Visual / widget opportunities | Source pages |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

No extra elaboration guidance beyond the official outcome was identified for this individual line. | `#FAS2`, `#HOOKE`, `#Mechanics1`, `#HookesLaw`, `#Elasticity` | `#FAS2`, `#HOOKE`, `#Mechanics1`, `#HookesLaw`, `#Elasticity`, `#WorkedSolution`, `#ExamTechnique`, `#SectionA` | Elastic string/spring force-extension graph with modelling assumptions. | Spec p. 13; Elaboration p. 5 |
| FAS2-HOOKE-LO002 | demonstrate understanding of and use the modelling assumptions in problems involving the application of Hooke's law, including familiarity with the idea of elastic limits | No additional elaboration guidance found in the supplied Elaboration Document for this individual learning outcome. | Understand and use the modelling assumptions in problems involving the application of Hooke's law, including familiarity with the idea of elastic limits | Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary. #### FAS2-HOOKE-LO001: Use Hooke's law as a model, relating the force in an elastic string or spring to the extension or compression, and understand the term modulus of elasticity

## Topic: Work and energy


**Applied section:** Section A: Mechanics 1

### Topic Summary

Work done by constant and variable forces, kinetic energy, gravitational potential energy, elastic potential energy and energy principles.

# FA21: Pure Mathematics (A2 1)
## Unit Overview
**Role in course:** Compulsory A2 pure unit for A level Further Mathematics.

**Assessment details:** External written examination; students answer all questions; 7-12 questions worth 150 raw marks.

**Prerequisite / assumption note:** Assessment guidance states that this unit assumes all CCEA GCE Mathematics units and Further Mathematics AS 1.

**Broad content areas:** Proof, further algebra and functions, complex numbers, further calculus, polar co-ordinates, hyperbolic functions and differential equations.

**Important student-facing comments:** This unit is where Further Pure becomes structurally different: induction, Maclaurin series, De Moivre, advanced integration and differential equations become central.

**Links to overarching themes:** mathematical language, proof/reasoning, modelling, diagrams, technology, communication and synoptic connections.

## Topic: Proof

### Topic Summary

Mathematical induction in contexts such as series, divisibility and powers of matrices.

### Learning Outcomes Table

| LO ID | Specification learning outcome | Elaboration guidance | Student-friendly meaning | Required depth / boundaries | Suggested lesson tags | Suggested question-bank tags | Visual / widget opportunities | Source pages |

Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure. | `#FA21`, `#PROOF`, `#Proof`, `#Induction` | `#FA21`, `#PROOF`, `#Proof`, `#Induction`, `#WorkedSolution`, `#ExamTechnique` | Induction ladder diagram showing base case and inductive step. | Spec p. 20; Elaboration p. 12 |

### Expanded Learning Outcome Notes

#### FA21-CN-LO001: Understand De Moivre's theorem and use it to find multiple angle formulae and sums of series

**Official specification wording:**  
demonstrate understanding of De Moivre's theorem and use it to find multiple angle formulae and sums of series

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Includes use of identities such as $z+1/z=2\cos\theta$ and $z-1/z=2i\sin\theta$ to find $\cos n\theta$, $\sin n\theta$ and $\tan n\theta$, and to express powers of trigonometric functions in terms of multiple angles.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Understand De Moivre's theorem and use it to find multiple angle formulae and sums of series

**What students should be able to do:**
- convert between trigonometric and exponential complex forms
- use De Moivre to derive trigonometric identities
- place nth roots geometrically on the Argand diagram

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary and apply the elaboration guidance exactly. Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
FAS1 complex numbers, radians, trigonometric identities and series.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#FA21`, `#CN`, `#ComplexNumbers`, `#DeMoivre`, `#RootsOfUnity`, `#WorkedSolution`, `#ExamTechnique`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
Regular polygon of nth roots on an Argand diagram.

**Links to other learning outcomes:**  
FA21-CN-LO002


**Source reference:**  
Specification: p. 20-21  
Elaboration Document: p. 13

### Expanded Learning Outcome Notes

#### FA21-FCALC-LO001: Evaluate improper integrals where either the integrand is undefined at a value in the range of integration or the range of integration extends to infinity

**Official specification wording:**  
evaluate improper integrals where either the integrand is undefined at a value in the range of integration or the range of integration extends to infinity

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Examples include $\int_0^9 x^{-1/2}\,dx$ and $\int_2^\infty x^{-2}\,dx$.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Evaluate improper integrals where either the integrand is undefined at a value in the range of integration or the range of integration extends to infinity

**What students should be able to do:**
- convert improper integrals into limiting processes
- choose integration techniques based on structure
- derive or apply reduction formulae clearly

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary and apply the elaboration guidance exactly. Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
A level Mathematics differentiation, integration by substitution, integration by parts and partial fractions.

Find and use an integrating factor to solve differential equations of the form $\frac{dy}{dx}+P(x)y=Q(x)$ and recognise when it is appropriate to do so

- use auxiliary equations and discriminants
- interpret constants and solutions in modelling contexts

# FA22: Applied Mathematics (A2 2)
## Unit Overview
**Role in course:** Compulsory A2 applied unit for A level Further Mathematics, with students answering two specified sections.

**Assessment details:** External written examination; 7-12 questions worth 150 raw marks. Students answer two of four sections: A and B, A and C, A and D, or C and D. Each chosen section is worth 50% of this unit.

**Prerequisite / assumption note:** Assumes all CCEA GCE Mathematics units and Further Mathematics AS 1 and A2 1.

**Broad content areas:** Mechanics 1, Mechanics 2, Statistics, and Discrete and Decision Mathematics.

**Important student-facing comments:** A2 applied work is more synoptic and more model-heavy. Mechanics 2 in this unit depends on AS2 Mechanics 1 and A2 Mechanics 1.

**Links to overarching themes:** mathematical language, proof/reasoning, modelling, diagrams, technology, communication and synoptic connections.

## Topic: Simple harmonic motion


**Applied section:** Section A: Mechanics 1

### Topic Summary

Standard SHM results, the SHM differential equation and spring/string/pendulum oscillations.

#### FA22-FCOM-LO001: Find the centre of mass of laminae and solids, including the use of calculus

**Official specification wording:**  
find the centre of mass of laminae and solids, including the use of calculus

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Proof of standard results for a solid cone and solid hemisphere only may be required. A table of standard results may be used. Calculus may include content from AS and A2 Mathematics Pure units and AS and A2 Further Mathematics Pure units.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Find the centre of mass of laminae and solids, including the use of calculus

**What students should be able to do:**
- use integration to derive or apply centre-of-mass formulae
- combine standard solid and lamina results
- analyse stability through vertical line of action

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary and apply the elaboration guidance exactly. Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure.

FA22 Centre of mass, integration, moments and statics.

### Expanded Learning Outcome Notes

#### FA22-LINCOMB-LO001: Understand and use the expressions for $E(aX+bY)$ and $\operatorname{Var}(aX+bY)$, where $X$ and $Y$ are independent random variables

**Official specification wording:**  
demonstrate understanding of and use the expressions for $E(aX+bY)$ and $\operatorname{Var}(aX+bY)$, where $X$ and $Y$ are independent random variables

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Proofs will not be required. Students are expected to know that if independent $X\sim N(\mu_X,\sigma_X^2)$ and $Y\sim N(\mu_Y,\sigma_Y^2)$, then $aX\pm bY\sim N(a\mu_X\pm b\mu_Y,a^2\sigma_X^2+b^2\sigma_Y^2)$.

**What this means in plain English:**  
Understand and use the expressions for $E(aX+bY)$ and $\operatorname{Var}(aX+bY)$, where $X$ and $Y$ are independent random variables

**What students should be able to do:**
- combine means linearly
- combine variances correctly under independence
- identify the distribution of sums and multiples of normal variables

**Required depth and boundaries:**  
Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary and apply the elaboration guidance exactly. Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure.

**Useful prerequisites:**  
Normal distribution, expectation, variance and algebra of random variables.

**Typical exam/question-bank tags:**  
`#FA22`, `#LINCOMB`, `#Statistics`, `#NormalDistribution`, `#LinearCombinations`, `#WorkedSolution`, `#ExamTechnique`, `#SectionC`

**Possible visuals or interactive supports:**  
Random-variable transformation table for $aX+bY$.

- choose degrees of freedom correctly
- combine classes and apply Yates' correction when required

#### FA22-CHI2-LO003: Use a $\chi^2$ test with the appropriate number of degrees of freedom to test for independence in a contingency table

**Official specification wording:**  
use a $\chi^2$ test with the appropriate number of degrees of freedom to test for independence in a contingency table

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
Rows or columns, as appropriate, should be combined so that each expected frequency is at least 5. Yates' correction should be used in the special case of a $2\times2$ table.

Use a $\chi^2$ test with the appropriate number of degrees of freedom to test for independence in a contingency table

- calculate expected frequencies from a hypothesis
- choose degrees of freedom correctly
- combine classes and apply Yates' correction when required

Use the official wording as the syllabus boundary and apply the elaboration guidance exactly. Do not extend the topic beyond the stated examples, inclusions and exclusions unless a question provides extra structure.

#### FA22-GROUP-LO001: Understand and work with symmetry groups, including the cyclic group $C_n$, the dihedral group $D_{2n}$ and the symmetry groups of the cube, octahedron and tetrahedron

demonstrate understanding of and work with symmetry groups, including the cyclic group $C_n$, the dihedral group $D_{2n}$ and the symmetry groups of the cube, octahedron and tetrahedron

**Elaboration document guidance:**  
No additional elaboration guidance found in the supplied Elaboration Document for this individual learning outcome.

## 9. Suggested Lesson Creation Order
### FAS1 (AS 1)
1. **Further algebra and functions** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
2. **Complex numbers** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
3. **Matrices** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
4. **Vectors** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.

### FAS2 (AS 2)
1. **Hooke's law** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
2. **Work and energy** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
3. **Power** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
4. **Circular motion** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
5. **Further particle equilibrium** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
6. **Resultant and relative velocity** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
7. **Further circular motion** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
8. **Gravitation** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
9. **Dimensions** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
10. **Sampling** - Section C: Statistics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
11. **Probability** - Section C: Statistics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
12. **Statistical distributions** - Section C: Statistics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
13. **Bivariate distributions** - Section C: Statistics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
14. **Group theory** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
15. **Graph theory** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
16. **Algorithms on graphs** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
17. **Recurrence relationships** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
18. **Boolean algebra** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.

### FA21 (A2 1)
1. **Proof** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
2. **Further algebra and functions** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
3. **Complex numbers** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
4. **Further calculus** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
5. **Polar co-ordinates** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
6. **Hyperbolic functions** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.
7. **Differential equations** - Builds the next layer of required methods and notation.

### FA22 (A2 2)
1. **Simple harmonic motion** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
2. **Damped oscillations** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
3. **Centre of mass** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
4. **Frameworks** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
5. **Further circular motion** - Section A: Mechanics 1 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
6. **Further kinematics** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
7. **Further centre of mass** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
8. **Force systems in two dimensions** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
9. **Restitution** - Section B: Mechanics 2 topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
10. **Linear combinations of independent variables** - Section C: Statistics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
11. **Sampling and estimation** - Section C: Statistics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
12. **The t-distribution** - Section C: Statistics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
13. **$\chi^2$ tests** - Section C: Statistics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
14. **Counting** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
15. **Graph theory** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
16. **Algorithms on graphs** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
17. **Generating functions** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.
18. **Group theory** - Section D: Discrete and Decision Mathematics topic; keep optional-section routing clear.

---
## 11. Ambiguities, Extraction Issues, and Assumptions
- Page references use the printed page numbers shown inside the PDFs, not zero-based PDF page indices.
- Raw PDF extraction garbled some mathematical symbols and repeated letters. Formulae were normalised where the surrounding specification made the intended notation clear, for example $v=r\omega$, $P=Fv$, $a=r\omega^2$, $a=v^2/r$, $\ddot{x}=-\omega^2x$, and random-variable notation involving $X$ and $Y$.
- The A2 1 Pure prerequisite line in one extracted text location contained an extraction artefact around `A2 7`; the map uses the assessment guidance wording that A2 1 assumes all GCE Mathematics units and Further Mathematics AS 1.
- AS2 and A22 applied units are choice-based. The allowed section pairs are A and B, A and C, A and D, or C and D. This map lists every section so the portal can support any valid route.
- Where elaboration guidance visually applied to a topic cluster rather than one single line, it is attached to the most relevant LO or repeated in nearby notes.
- The document does not add extra mathematical content as official syllabus content. Student-friendly explanations, prerequisites, tags and visual suggestions are planning aids only.
